\displaystyle J\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle p^{\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon})]=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})]+\sum_{k=1}^{J-1}\varepsilon^{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[g_{k}]+\varepsilon^{J}\cdot{R}_{J}(x,\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\equiv p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)+\sum_{k=1}^{J-1}\varepsilon^{k}\cdot\Xi_{k}(x,\xi;\theta)+\varepsilon^{J}\cdot R_{J}(x,\xi;\theta),

(E.9)

where
\displaystyle g_{k},\,1\leq k\leq J-1
are explicitly defined via the formal Taylor expansion of
\displaystyle\varepsilon\mapsto\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon})
and the term
\displaystyle R_{J}(x,\xi;\theta)
is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle{R}_{J}(x,\xi;\theta)=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(1-\lambda)^{J-1}}{(J-1)!}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\frac{\partial^{J}}{\partial\eta^{J}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta})\Bigr]|_{\eta=\varepsilon\lambda}\,d\lambda.

(E.10)

Note that
\displaystyle\xi\mapsto p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)
represents the density of Gaussian random variable
\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}
whose covariance is non-degenerate under Conditions (
H3
)–(
H4
). In particular, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi_{1}(x,\xi;\theta)=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{j_{2}}^{i}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\Bigr]+\sum_{i=d_{R}+1}^{d}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\Bigr];

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi_{2}(x,\xi;\theta)=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigl\{\hat{V}_{0}V_{j}^{i}(x,\theta)\zeta_{0j,1}+\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\zeta_{j0,1}\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=d_{R}+1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigl\{\hat{V}_{0}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\eta_{0j,1}+\hat{V}_{j}\hat{V}_{0}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\eta_{j0,1}\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{j_{2}}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{3}}V_{j_{4}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\zeta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}\leq d_{R}\\
d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d\end{subarray}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{j_{2}}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{3}}\hat{V}_{j_{4}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\eta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{3}}\hat{V}_{j_{4}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\eta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\Bigr].

(E.11)

E.2.2
Step 2. Expression for Expanded Terms

We next consider the expression for the expanded terms
\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta),\,1\leq l\leq J-1
, and observe that they have the general form:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta)=\sum_{1\leq k\leq\nu(l)}\sum_{\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{k}}\nu_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}^{\widetilde{Y}}_{\alpha}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

(E.12)

where
\displaystyle\nu(l)\geq 1
is some integer,
\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}(\cdot,\theta)\in C_{b}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}),\,\theta\in\Theta
is given by the products of
\displaystyle V_{j}(\cdot,\theta),\;0\leq j\leq d_{R}
and theirs partial derivatives, and
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\widetilde{Y}}_{\alpha}(\xi;\theta)
is Hermite polynomial defined in (
3.16
) given in the main text. We here provide the representation for
\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta),\,l=1,2
, and then discuss the case
\displaystyle l\geq 3
. To obtain an analytic expression for
\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta),\,l=1,2
, we make use of the following result whose proof is found in Section
E.3.2
:

Lemma E.2
.

Let
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\leq d_{R}
and
\displaystyle 1\leq i_{1},i_{2}\leq d
. Under conditions (
H3
)–(
H4
), it holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{0j_{1},1}]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d}\Bigl\{\tfrac{1}{2}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{1\leq i_{2}\leq d_{R}}+\tfrac{1}{6}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d}\Bigr\}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta);

(E.13)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}0,1}]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d}\Bigl\{\tfrac{1}{2}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{1\leq i_{2}\leq d_{R}}+\tfrac{1}{3}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d}\Bigr\}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta);

(E.14)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{0j_{1},1}]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d}\Bigl\{\tfrac{1}{6}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{1\leq i_{2}\leq d_{R}}+\tfrac{1}{6}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d}\Bigr\}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta);

(E.15)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{j_{1}0,1}]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d}\Bigl\{\tfrac{1}{6}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{1\leq i_{2}\leq d_{R}}+\tfrac{1}{8}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d}\Bigr\}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta);

(E.16)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\bigr]=\sum_{i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{d}\biggl\{\tfrac{1}{2}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)V_{j_{2}}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{1\leq i_{2},i_{3}\leq d_{R}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{3}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)V_{j_{2}}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{{d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d,1\leq i_{3}\leq d_{R}}}+\tfrac{1}{6}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{{1\leq i_{2}\leq d_{R},d_{R}+1\leq i_{3}\leq d}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{8}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{d_{R}+1\leq i_{2},i_{3}\leq d}\biggr\}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta);

(E.17)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\zeta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\bigr]=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}+r_{1}(x,\xi;\theta);

(E.18)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\eta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}]=\tfrac{1}{12}\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}+r_{2}(x,\xi;\theta),

(E.19)

where the terms

\displaystyle r_{i}(x,\xi;\theta),\;i=1,2
are given in the form of

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{l=4,6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{l}\end{subarray}}v_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}^{\widetilde{Y}}_{\alpha}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

(E.20)

for some
\displaystyle v_{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
being of the same structure as given in (
E.12
).

Thus, applying Lemma
E.2
to (
E.2.1
), we obtain expression for
\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta),\,l=1,2
as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi_{1}(x,\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\biggl\{\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{j_{2}}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{1\leq i_{1}\leq d_{R}}+\tfrac{1}{3}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{d_{R}+1\leq i_{1}\leq d}\biggr\}\cdot\widetilde{\Xi}^{i_{1}}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(x,\xi;\theta),

(E.21)

where

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{\Xi}^{i_{1}}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(x,\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)V_{j_{2}}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\,\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d\\
1\leq i_{3}\leq d_{R}\end{subarray}}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)V_{j_{2}}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\,\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{2}\leq d_{R}\\
d_{R}+1\leq i_{3}\leq d\end{subarray}}V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{8}\sum_{i_{2},i_{3}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

and

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi_{2}(x,\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}\Bigl(\hat{V}_{k}V_{0}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)+\hat{V}_{0}V_{k}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\Bigr)V_{k}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{4}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{{d_{R}}}\hat{V}_{k_{1}}V_{k_{2}}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{k_{1}}V_{k_{2}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}\leq d_{R}\\
d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d\end{subarray}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{3}\hat{V}_{k}V_{0}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)+\tfrac{1}{6}\hat{V}_{0}V_{k}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\Bigr)\hat{V}_{k}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}\leq d_{R}\\
d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d\end{subarray}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}V_{k}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\Bigl(\hat{V}_{0}\hat{V}_{k}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)+\hat{V}_{k}\hat{V}_{0}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\Bigr)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}\leq d_{R}\\
d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d\end{subarray}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{{d_{R}}}\hat{V}_{k_{1}}V_{k_{2}}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{k_{1}}\hat{V}_{k_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{k}V_{0}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{6}\hat{V}_{0}\hat{V}_{k}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)+\tfrac{1}{8}\hat{V}_{k}\hat{V}_{0}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\Bigr)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{24}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{{d_{R}}}\hat{V}_{k_{1}}\hat{V}_{k_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{k_{1}}\hat{V}_{k_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=4,6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{l}\end{subarray}}w_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\times\mathcal{H}^{\widetilde{Y}}_{\alpha}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

(E.22)

with
\displaystyle w_{\alpha}
having the same structure as
\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}
in (
E.12
).

We consider the terms
\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta)
for
\displaystyle l\geq 3
, and observe that they have the general form as (
E.12
).
First, from
\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon}
given in (
E.7
), each of the terms in
\displaystyle{\Xi}_{l}(x,\xi;\theta),\,l\geq 3
is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle v_{\beta}^{\alpha}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})I_{\beta}(1)\Bigr]

for some multi-indices
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{k},\;k\geq 1
and
\displaystyle\beta\in\{0,1,\ldots,d_{R}\}^{\nu},\;\nu\geq 1
, and
\displaystyle v_{\beta}^{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
being the same structure as
\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}
in (
E.12
) under condition (
H2
).
We note that the multiple stochastic integral
\displaystyle I_{\beta}(1)
comes from application of Itô’s formula to the products of combinations of the following random variables:

B_{k_{1},1},\quad\widetilde{B}_{k_{2},2},\quad I_{(k_{3},0)}(1),\quad I_{(0,k_{4})}(1),\qquad 0\leq k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\leq d_{R}.

Then, iterative use of duality formula (
E.2
) (in particular, Lemma 2.5 in
Iguchi and Yamada 2021b
) yields

\displaystyle\displaystyle v_{\beta}^{\alpha}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})I_{\beta}(1)\Bigr]=\sum_{\gamma\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{n(\beta)}}v_{\beta}^{\alpha}(x,\theta)v_{\gamma}(x,\theta)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\gamma}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\Bigr],

(E.23)

for some
\displaystyle v_{\gamma}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
again characterised as
\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}
in (
E.12
), where
\displaystyle n(\beta)
is the number of non-zero elements in
\displaystyle\beta
. Thus, we conclude that
\displaystyle{\Xi}_{l}(x,\xi;\theta),\,l\geq 3
, admits the general form (
E.12
) from the following formula (e.g., see Theorem 9.2 in
Ikeda and Watanabe 2014
):

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial^{\beta}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})]=(-1)^{|\beta|}\partial^{\xi}_{\beta}p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)=\mathcal{H}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\cdot p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),\quad\beta\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{l},\;l\geq 1.

(E.24)

E.2.3
Step 3. Specification of the Residual Term

We consider the term
\displaystyle R_{J}(x,\xi;\theta)
specified as (
E.10
). Each of the terms in
\displaystyle\textstyle{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\frac{\partial^{J}}{\partial\eta^{J}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta})]}
is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta})G_{\alpha}^{\eta}(x;\theta)\Bigr],

where
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{k},\;k=\nu_{J},\ldots,J
for some integer
\displaystyle\nu_{J}<J
, and
\displaystyle G_{\alpha}^{\eta}(x;\theta)\in\mathbb{D}^{\infty}
is given by products of
\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}^{(\beta_{1}),\alpha_{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}^{(\beta_{k}),\alpha_{k}}
with
\displaystyle\beta\in\{1,2,3\}^{k}
. In particular, under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
), there exists a constant
\displaystyle c>0
independent of
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
such that
\displaystyle\|G_{\alpha}^{\eta}(x;\theta)\|_{k,p}\leq c
for any
\displaystyle k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\displaystyle p\geq 1
. Since
\displaystyle\{\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}\}_{\eta\in(0,1)}
is non-degenerate uniformly in
\displaystyle\eta\in(0,1)
from Lemma
E.1
, one can apply the Malliavin integration by parts and obtain the following estimate due to the upper bound (
E.1
):

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta})G_{\alpha}^{\eta}(x;\theta)\Bigr]\Bigl|=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}\geq\xi}H_{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k},1,\ldots,d)}(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta},G_{\alpha}^{\eta}(x;\theta))\Bigr]\Bigl|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|H_{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k},1,\ldots,d)}(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta},G_{\alpha}^{\eta}(x;\theta))\bigr\|_{p}\leq c\,\|(\det\sigma^{\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}})^{-1}\|_{q}^{m}\leq C,

for some
\displaystyle p,q\geq 2
and positive constants
\displaystyle C,c,m
independent of
\displaystyle\xi,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\theta\in\Theta
.
Thus, there exists a constant
\displaystyle C>0
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{x,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\theta\in\Theta}\bigl|R_{J}(x,\xi;\theta)\bigr|\leq C.

E.2.4
Final Step. Complete Form of Density Expansion

Finally, substituting
\displaystyle\sqrt{\Delta}
for
\displaystyle\varepsilon
in the formula (
E.8
) with (
E.2.1
), (
E.12
), (
E.2.2
) and (
E.22
), we obtain the density expansion (
3.17
) in the main text. We note that

\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta)|_{\xi=m_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(y)}
corresponds to

\displaystyle\Psi_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\times p_{\Delta}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}(x,y;\theta)
,
\displaystyle 1\leq l\leq J-1
. The proof of Lemma
3.1
is now complete.

E.3
Proof of Technical Results for Lemma
3.1

E.3.1
Proof of Lemma
E.1

We will show that, for any
\displaystyle t>0
,
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\textstyle{\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\|(\det\sigma^{\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon}})^{-1}\|_{p}<\infty}

for all
\displaystyle p\in(1,\infty)
. The Malliavin derivative of
\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon}
is given via

\displaystyle D_{k,s}\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon}\equiv F_{k,s}^{(1)}+F_{k,s}^{(2),\varepsilon}
,
\displaystyle 1\leq k\leq d_{R}
,
\displaystyle s>0
, where

\displaystyle\displaystyle F_{k,s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}D_{k,s}\widetilde{Y}_{R}\\
D_{k,s}\widetilde{Y}_{S}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}V_{R,k}(x,\theta)\times\mathbf{1}_{s\in[0,1]}\\[2.84544pt]
\hat{V}_{k}V_{S,0}(x,\theta)(1-s)\times\mathbf{1}_{s\in[0,1]}\end{bmatrix},\ \ F_{k,s}^{(2),\varepsilon}=\sum_{l=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{l}\cdot D_{k,s}\widetilde{Y}^{(l)}.

Under conditions (
H3
)–(
H4
), there exists a constant
\displaystyle c>0
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{|\xi|=1,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigl\langle Q(s)\xi,\,\xi\bigr\rangle\geq c,\qquad 0<s<1,

(E.25)

where
\displaystyle Q(s)
is a
\displaystyle d\times d
matrix with elements given as

Q_{ij}(s)=\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}F_{k,s}^{(1),i}F_{k,s}^{(1),j},\qquad 1\leq i,j\leq d.

Notice that the matrix
\displaystyle Q(s)
is independent of
\displaystyle\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and the positive definiteness holds uniformly in
\displaystyle\varepsilon
. Furthermore, under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
), there exists a constant
\displaystyle C>0
independent of
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta
such that for any
\displaystyle p\in(1,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{s\leq 1,\;\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[|F_{k,s}^{(2),\varepsilon}|^{p}]\leq C.

(E.26)

From Lemma 5 (or equivalently Proposition 9) in
Bally 2003
, (
E.25
) and (
E.26
) lead to the following result. For every
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta
, we have
\displaystyle\textstyle{\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\|(\det\sigma^{\widetilde{Y}^{\varepsilon}})^{-1}\|_{p}<\infty}
for any
\displaystyle p\in(1,\infty)
. The proof is now complete.

E.3.2
Proof of Lemma
E.2

Derivation of (
E.13
):
The duality formula (
E.2
) and (
E.24
) yield

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}sdB_{j,s}\Bigr]=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}D_{j,s}\widetilde{Y}^{i_{2}}sds\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigr]+\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)+\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

where we used

\displaystyle\displaystyle D_{j_{2},s}\widetilde{Y}=\begin{bmatrix}V_{R,j_{2}}(x,\theta)\times\mathbf{1}_{s\in[0,1]}\\
\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{S,0}(x,\theta)(1-s)\times\mathbf{1}_{s\in[0,1]}\end{bmatrix}.

(E.27)

Derivation of (
E.14
):
The duality formula (
E.2
), (
E.24
) and (
E.27
) yield

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}B_{j,s}ds\Bigr]=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)dB_{j,s}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigr]+\tfrac{1}{3}\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)+\tfrac{1}{3}\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta).

Derivation of (
E.15
):

We have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{0j,1}]=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j0,1}]-\tfrac{1}{3}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j,1}],

(E.28)

and

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j,1}]=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}D_{j,s}\widetilde{Y}^{i_{2}}ds]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j}^{i_{2}}(x;\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta).

(E.29)

Using (
E.14
) and (
E.29
), we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{0j,1}]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)+\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta).

Derivation of (
E.16
):
Making use of (
E.14
) and (
E.29
), we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{j0,1}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j0,1}]-\tfrac{1}{12}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j,1}]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}V_{j}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)+\tfrac{1}{8}\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\hat{V}_{j}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta).

Derivation of (
E.17
):
Recall: for
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq d_{R}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}=\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl\{B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{2},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{2}}\bigr\}+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{j_{1},1}\widetilde{B}_{j_{2},1}\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}<j_{2}}-\tfrac{1}{2}B_{j_{2},1}\widetilde{B}_{j_{1},1}\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}>j_{2}}.

(E.30)

Since
\displaystyle\widetilde{B}
and
\displaystyle B
are independent, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigl\{B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{2},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{2}}\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\Bigl\{\int_{0}^{1}B_{j_{1},s}dB_{j_{2},s}+\int_{0}^{1}B_{j_{2},s}dB_{j_{1},s}\Bigr\}\Bigr].

The duality formula (
E.2
) and the Malliavin derivative (
E.27
) give

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}B_{j_{1},s}dB_{j_{2},s}\Bigr]=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}D_{j_{2},s}\widetilde{Y}^{i_{2}}B_{j_{1},s}ds\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}B_{j_{1},s}ds\Bigr]V_{j_{2}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i_{2}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)B_{j_{1},s}ds\Bigr]\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{d_{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{3}}\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\Bigr]V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)V_{j_{2}}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{R}+1\leq i_{2}\leq d\\
1\leq i_{3}\leq d_{R}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{3}}\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\Bigr]\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)V_{j_{2}}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{2}\leq d_{R}\\
d_{R}+1\leq i_{3}\leq d\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{3}}\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\Bigr]V_{j_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{8}\sum_{i_{2},i_{3}=d_{R}+1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{3}}\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{1}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\Bigr]\hat{V}_{j_{1}}V_{0}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{j_{2}}V_{0}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta),

where we used

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}B_{j_{1},s}ds=\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)dB_{j_{1},s},\ \ \int_{0}^{1}(1-s)B_{j_{1},s}ds=\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)^{2}dB_{j_{1},s}.

Thus, we obtain (
E.17
).

Derivation of (
E.18
):

From (
E.30
) we have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\zeta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\bigr]=\tfrac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigl\{B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{2},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{2}}\bigr\}\bigl\{B_{j_{3},1}B_{j_{4},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{3}=j_{4}}\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{3},1}\bigr]\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1},j_{3}<j_{2}=j_{4}}-\tfrac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{4},1}\bigr]\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1},j_{4}<j_{2}=j_{3}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad-\tfrac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j_{2},1}B_{j_{3},1}\bigr]\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{2},j_{3}<j_{1}=j_{4}}+\tfrac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j_{2},1}B_{j_{4},1}\bigr]\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{2},j_{4}<j_{1}=j_{3}}.

Itô’s formula yields

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl\{B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{2},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{2}}\bigr\}\bigl\{B_{j_{3},1}B_{j_{4},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{3}=j_{4}}\bigr\}=\bigl\{I_{(j_{1},j_{2})}(1)+I_{(j_{2},j_{1})}(1)\bigr\}\bigl\{I_{(j_{3},j_{4})}(1)+I_{(j_{4},j_{3})}(1)\bigr\},

and

\displaystyle\displaystyle I_{(j_{1},j_{2})}(1)I_{(j_{3},j_{4})}(1)=I_{(j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4})}(1)+I_{(j_{1},j_{3},j_{2},j_{4})}(1)+I_{(j_{1},j_{3},j_{4},j_{2})}(1)+I_{(j_{3},j_{1},j_{4},j_{2})}(1)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+I_{(0,j_{4},j_{2})}(1)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3}}+I_{(j_{1},0,j_{4})}(1)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{2}=j_{3}}+I_{(0,j_{4},j_{2})}(1)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+I_{(j_{3},0,j_{2})}(1)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{4}}+I_{(j_{1},j_{3},0)}(1)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{2}=j_{4}}+I_{(j_{3},j_{1},0)}(1)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{2}=j_{4}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}.

(E.31)

Making use of (
E.3.2
) and the duality formula (
E.2
), we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigl\{B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{2},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{2}}\bigr\}\bigl\{B_{j_{3},1}B_{j_{4},1}-\mathbf{1}_{j_{3}=j_{4}}\bigr\}\bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad=\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\times\bigl\{\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}+\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{4},j_{2}=j_{3}}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{k=2,4}\sum_{\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{k}}w_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\times(-1)^{|\alpha|}\,\partial^{\xi}_{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k},i_{1},i_{2})}p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

where
\displaystyle w_{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
is of the same structure as the one described for
\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}
in (
E.20
) under condition (
H2
). We also have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{3},1}\bigr]\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1},j_{3}<j_{2}=j_{4}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigr]\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3}<j_{2}=j_{4}}+\ \sum_{\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{2}}\widetilde{w}_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3}<j_{2}=j_{4}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{2}}\widetilde{w}_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\times(-1)^{|\alpha|}\,\partial^{\xi}_{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k},i_{1},i_{2})}p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

with some
\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
having the same property as the above
\displaystyle w_{\alpha}
, where we used
\displaystyle B_{j_{1},1}B_{j_{2},1}=I_{(j_{1},j_{2})}(1)+I_{(j_{2},j_{1})}(1)+\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{2}}
for
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq d_{R}
in the first equality. Thus, we obtain (
E.18
).

Derivation of (
E.19
):

From the definition of
\displaystyle\textstyle{\eta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}=\frac{1}{3}\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}j_{2},1}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\eta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\eta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\bigr]=\tfrac{1}{9}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\zeta_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\zeta_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\bigr]+\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{3}j_{4},1}\bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{18}\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}+\sum_{k=2,4}\sum_{\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{k}}v_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\cdot(-1)^{|\alpha|}\partial^{\xi}_{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k},i_{1},i_{2})}p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad+\tfrac{1}{36}\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{12}\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}+\sum_{k=2,4}\sum_{\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{k}}v_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\cdot(-1)^{|\alpha|}\partial^{\xi}_{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k},i_{1},i_{2})}p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

where we used
\displaystyle\textstyle{\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}j_{2},1}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{3}j_{4},1}]=\frac{1}{36}\times\mathbf{1}_{j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4}}}
and (
E.18
).

Appendix F
Proof of Proposition
4.1

F.1
Proof of Properties (i)–(iii)

We exploit the density expansion (
3.17
) obtained in Lemma
3.1
. Property (iii) holds immediately by identifying
\displaystyle R^{J}_{2}(x,y;\theta)
with the residual term
\displaystyle R^{J}(x,y;\theta)
in Lemma
3.1
. We make use of the following notation: for a multi-index
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{l},\,l\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\alpha\|_{R}\equiv(\mathrm{\#\,of\,elements\,taking\,in\,the\,rough\,coordinates\,}\{1,\ldots,d_{R}\});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\alpha\|_{S}\equiv(\mathrm{\#\,of\,elements\,taking\,in\,the\,smooth\,coordinates\,}\{d_{R}+1,\ldots,d\}).

To prove Properties (i)–(ii), we first introduce the following result about the expression for
\displaystyle\Psi_{3}
in the density expansion (
3.17
):

Lemma F.1
.

Let
\displaystyle\Delta>0
,
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
. It holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Psi_{3}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)=\Psi_{3}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)+\widetilde{\Psi}_{3}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),

(F.1)

where
\displaystyle\Psi_{3}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
is given in (
4.12
) in the main text, and each of the terms in
\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{3}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle v_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),

(F.2)

for a multi-index
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{l},\;l\geq 3
,
and the function
\displaystyle v_{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}

is determined from the products of
\displaystyle V_{j}(\cdot;\theta),\;0\leq j\leq d_{R}
and theirs partial derivatives.

We provide the proof in Section
F.1.1
. To determine the residual term
\displaystyle R_{1}^{J}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
, we introduce the following result whose proof is contained in Section
F.1.2
:

Lemma F.2
.

Let
\displaystyle\Delta>0
,
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
and
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{l},\,l\geq 1
. For any
\displaystyle\varphi\in C_{b}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, there exist a constant
\displaystyle C>0
and
\displaystyle q\geq 1
so that:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)p_{\Delta}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}(x,y;\theta)dy\right|\leq C(1+|x|^{q})\cdot\sqrt{\Delta^{\|\alpha\|_{R}+3\|\alpha\|_{S}}}.

(F.3)

Since
\displaystyle\Psi_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),\,l\geq 4
, involves Hermite polynomials
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
with
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{j},\,j\geq 2
from the expression of
\displaystyle\Xi_{l}(x,\xi;\theta)
in the expansion (
E.2.1
), Lemma
F.2
yields:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l=4}^{J-1}\Delta^{\tfrac{l}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)\,\Psi_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\,p_{\Delta}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}(x,y;\theta)dy\right|\leq C(1+|x|^{q})\Delta^{3},

(F.4)

for some
\displaystyle C>0
and
\displaystyle q\geq 1
, which indicates that
\displaystyle\Delta^{{l}/{2}}\Psi_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),\,4\leq l\leq J-1,
are included in the residual
\displaystyle R_{1}^{J}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
. We also write: for
\displaystyle\Delta>0
,
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Psi_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\equiv\Psi_{l}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)+\widetilde{\Psi_{l}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),\quad l=1,2,

where
\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi_{l}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
is explicitly determined from the definition of
\displaystyle\Psi_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
and
\displaystyle\Psi_{l}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
in Lemma
3.1
and Proposition
4.1
given in the main text, respectively. Notice that every term in
\displaystyle\Delta^{{l}/{2}}\cdot\widetilde{\Psi}_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),\,1\leq l\leq 3
, contains

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Delta^{\tfrac{l}{2}}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),\quad\alpha\in\{1\ldots,d\}^{j},\ \ j\geq 1,

(F.5)

with
\displaystyle l
and
\displaystyle\alpha
satisfying
\displaystyle l+\|\alpha\|_{R}+3\|\alpha\|_{S}\geq 6
. Thus, Lemma
F.2
yields:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l=1}^{3}\Delta^{\tfrac{l}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)\,\widetilde{\Psi}_{l}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\,p_{\Delta}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}(x,y;\theta)dy\right|\leq C(1+|x|^{q})\Delta^{3},

(F.6)

for some
\displaystyle C>0
and
\displaystyle q\geq 1
. The proof is now complete.

F.1.1
Proof of Lemma
F.1

Notice from the proof of Lemma
3.1
in Section
E.2
that

\Psi_{3}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)p_{\Delta}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}(x,y;\theta)=\Xi_{3}(x,\xi;\theta)|_{\xi=m_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(y)},

where
\displaystyle\Xi_{3}(x,\xi;\theta)
is determined from the expansion (
E.2.1
), and given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi_{3}(x,\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{0}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\Bigr]+\widetilde{\Xi}_{3}(x,\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{0}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i)}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)+\widetilde{\Xi}_{3}(x,\xi;\theta),

(F.7)

where we have applied the formula (
E.24
) to the first term in the right hand side of (
F.1.1
), and we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{\Xi}_{3}(x,\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\widetilde{Y}^{(1),i_{1}}\widetilde{Y}^{(2),i_{2}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{3!}\sum_{1\leq i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\leq d}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial_{i_{1}}\partial_{i_{2}}\partial_{i_{3}}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})\widetilde{Y}^{(1),i_{1}}\widetilde{Y}^{(1),i_{2}}\widetilde{Y}^{(1),i_{3}}\Bigr].

(F.8)

We will now show that each of the terms in

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Xi}_{3}(x,\xi;\theta)

is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta),

(F.9)

where the multi-index
\displaystyle\alpha
and
\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}(\cdot,\cdot)
are the same as those in (
F.2
). From (
F.1.1
), each of the terms in

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Xi}_{3}(x,\xi;\theta)
is written as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\delta_{\xi}(\widetilde{Y})I_{\beta}(1)\Bigr]v_{\beta}^{\alpha}(x,\theta),

(F.10)

for some multi-indices
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^{k},\;k=2,3
and
\displaystyle\beta\in\{0,1,\ldots,d_{R}\}^{\nu},\;\nu\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle k+n(\beta)\geq 3
, and
\displaystyle v_{\beta}^{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
being the same structure as
\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha}
in (
F.2
). We recall
\displaystyle n(\beta)
is the number of non-zero elements in the multi-index
\displaystyle\beta
. Thus, applying (
E.23
) and (
E.24
) to (
F.10
), we conclude that the term (
F.10
) is of the form (
F.9
). The proof is now complete.

F.1.2
Proof of Lemma
F.2

It follows from the definition of Hermite polynomials and the integration by parts that for
\displaystyle\varphi\in C_{p}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}^{\widetilde{Y}}\bigl(m_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(y);\theta\bigr)\times\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta^{d_{R}+3d_{S}}}}p^{\widetilde{Y}}\bigl(m_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(y);\theta\bigr)dy

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi\bigl(h_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(\xi)\bigr)(-1)^{|\alpha|}\times\partial^{\xi}_{\alpha}p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)d\xi

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Delta^{\|\alpha\|_{R}+3\|\alpha\|_{S}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial^{z}_{\alpha}\varphi(z)|_{z=h_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(\xi)}\times p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)d\xi

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Delta^{\|\alpha\|_{R}+3\|\alpha\|_{S}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\varphi\bigl(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG},x}_{\Delta}\bigr)\bigr],

(F.11)

where we have used the change of variables in the second line, and the function
\displaystyle h_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is defined as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\xi=(\xi_{R},\xi_{S})\mapsto h_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(\xi)=\begin{bmatrix}x_{R}+V_{R,0}(x,\beta)\Delta+\sqrt{\Delta}\xi_{R}\\[5.69046pt]
x_{S}+V_{S,0}(x,\gamma)\Delta+\tfrac{\Delta^{2}}{2}\hat{V}_{0}V_{S,0}(x,\theta)+\sqrt{\Delta^{3}}\xi_{S}\end{bmatrix}.

(F.12)

Thus, from the property of the test function
\displaystyle\varphi\in C_{p}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and (
F.11
), we have that:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)p_{\Delta}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}(x,y;\theta)dy\right|=\sqrt{\Delta^{\|\alpha\|_{R}+3\|\alpha\|_{S}}}\cdot\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\partial^{\alpha}\varphi\bigl(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG},x}_{\Delta}\bigr)\bigr]\Bigr|\leq C(1+|x|^{q}),

for some constants
\displaystyle C>0
and
\displaystyle q\geq 1
, where we exploited the following bound: under Assumptions (
H1
)–(
H2
), for any

\displaystyle\Delta>0
,
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
, and
\displaystyle p\geq 1
, there exists
\displaystyle C>0
so that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\bigl|\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG},x}_{\Delta}\bigr|^{p}\Bigr]\leq C(1+|x|^{p}).

(F.13)

The proof of Lemma
F.2
is now complete.

F.2
Proof of Property (iv)

From the properties of Hermite polynomials, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\,p^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)\,d\xi=0,\qquad\theta\in\Theta.

Due to the definition of
\displaystyle\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)
involving
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}^{\widetilde{Y}}(\xi;\theta)|_{\xi=m_{x,\theta,\sqrt{\Delta}}(y)}
, change of variables gives:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\,p^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}_{\Delta}(x,y;\theta)dy=0,

and now the proof is complete.

Appendix G
Proof of Proposition
4.2

A Taylor expansion of the exponential function gives:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\exp\left(K(z)\right)-(1+z)=\sum_{i=7}^{\nu}c_{i}z^{i}+g(z)\tfrac{z^{8}}{7!},\ \ z\in\mathbb{R},

(G.1)

for some integer
\displaystyle\nu>7
and constants
\displaystyle c_{i}
, where

\displaystyle\displaystyle g(z):=\int_{0}^{1}\exp\left(sK(z)\right)ds.

Notice that given the definition of the function
\displaystyle z\mapsto K(z)
, there exists a constant
\displaystyle C>0
so that
\displaystyle\textstyle{\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}|g(z)|\leq C}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)p^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}_{\Delta}(x,y;\theta)\left(1+\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\right)dy-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II}}_{\Delta}(x,y;\theta)dy\right|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=7}^{\nu}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)c_{i}\bigl\{\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\bigr\}^{i}p^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}_{\Delta}(x,y;\theta)dy\right|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(y)g\bigl(\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\bigr)\tfrac{\bigl\{\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\bigr\}^{8}}{7!}p^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{LG}}}_{\Delta}(x,y;\theta)dy\right|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C(1+|x|^{q})\Delta^{3},

for some constants
\displaystyle C>0

\displaystyle q\geq 1
, where we made use of

\displaystyle\textstyle{\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigl|g\bigl(\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\bigr)\bigr|<\infty}
, of the polynomial growth of the test function
\displaystyle\varphi
, and of the following estimates:

•

Under conditions (
H1
)-(
H2
), there exist constants
\displaystyle C>0
and
\displaystyle q_{1},q_{2}\geq 1
independent of
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl|\Psi^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\bigr|\leq C(1+|x|^{q_{1}}+|y|^{q_{2}})\sqrt{\Delta}.

•

Under conditions (
H1
)-(
H2
), for
\displaystyle p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
\displaystyle C>0
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\bigl|\widetilde{X}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{LG},x}\bigr|^{p}\bigr]\leq C(1+|x|^{p}).

The proof is now complete.

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
4.3

Let
\displaystyle\Delta>0
,
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
. We have that

\displaystyle\displaystyle p_{e,\Delta}^{X}(x,y;\theta)-\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II},(M)}_{e,\Delta}(x,y;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad=\big(p_{e,\Delta}^{X}(x,y;\theta)-\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{I},(M)}_{e,\Delta}(x,y;\theta)\big)+\big(\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{I},(M)}_{e,\Delta}(x,y;\theta)-\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II},(M)}_{e,\Delta}(x,y;\theta)\big).

(H.1)

For the first term on the right-hand-side of (
H
), we obtain the upper bound (
4.20
) given in the main text under conditions (
H1
)–(
H3
) from
Iguchi and Yamada 2021a
. For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{I},(M)}_{e,\Delta}(x,y;\theta)-\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II},(M)}_{e,\Delta}(x,y;\theta)=E_{1}+E_{2},

(H.2)

where we have defined

\displaystyle\displaystyle E_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}\times(M-1)}}\Bigl\{\prod_{i=1}^{M-1}\bar{p}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\,\mathrm{I}}(x_{i-1},x_{i};\theta)\Bigr\}\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x_{M-1},y;\theta)\,dx_{1}\cdots dx_{M-1};

\displaystyle\displaystyle E_{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{M-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}\times(M-1)}}\Bigl\{\prod_{i=1}^{M-k-1}\bar{p}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\,\mathrm{I}}(x_{i-1},x_{i};\theta)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x_{M-k-1},x_{M-k};\theta)\Bigl\{\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{p}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\,\mathrm{II}}(x_{M-k+(i-1)},x_{M-k+i};\theta)\Bigr\}\,dx_{1}\cdots dx_{M-1}.

The following term is used in the above expressions:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{s}(z_{1},z_{2};\theta):=\bar{p}_{e,s}^{\,\mathrm{I}}(z_{1},z_{2};\theta)-\bar{p}_{e,s}^{\,\mathrm{II}}(z_{1},z_{2};\theta),\qquad s\geq 0,\;z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}},\;\theta\in\Theta.

(H.3)

We obtain the following bounds for
\displaystyle E_{1}
and
\displaystyle E_{2}
.

Lemma H.1
.

Assume that conditions (
H1
)–(
H3
) hold. There exist constants
\displaystyle C_{1},C_{2},c_{1},c_{2}>0
,
\displaystyle q\geq d_{R}/2
and non-decreasing functions
\displaystyle h_{1}(\cdot)
,
\displaystyle h_{2}(\cdot)
such that for any
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
,
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle|E_{1}|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h_{1}(\Delta)}{\Delta^{q}}e^{-c_{1}\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{\Delta}},

(H.4)

\displaystyle\displaystyle|E_{2}|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(M-1)(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h_{2}(\Delta)}{\Delta^{q}}e^{-c_{2}\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{\Delta}}.

(H.5)

We provide the proof in Section
H.1
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big|\,\bar{p}_{e,\Delta}^{\,\mathrm{I},(M)}(x,y;\theta)-\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II},(M)}_{e,\Delta}(x,y;\theta)\,\big|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq MC(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h(\Delta)}{\Delta^{q}}e^{-c\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{\Delta}}=\frac{C^{\prime}}{M^{2}}\frac{h(\Delta)}{\Delta^{q}}e^{-c\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{\Delta}},

where
\displaystyle h(\cdot)
is a non-decreasing function and
\displaystyle C,C^{\prime},c>0
are some constants independent of
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta,\Delta>0
and
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
. The proof is complete.

H.1
Proof of Lemma
H.1

H.1.1
Upper Bound (
H.4
)

Term
\displaystyle E_{1}
is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle E_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}E_{1,1}(x,x_{M-1};\theta)E_{1,2}(x_{M-1},y;\theta)dx_{M-1},

where

\displaystyle\displaystyle E_{1,1}(x,x_{M-1};\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}\times(M-2)}}\prod_{i=1}^{M-1}\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{I}}_{e,\delta_{M}}(x_{i-1},x_{i};\theta)dx_{1}\cdots dx_{M-2};

\displaystyle\displaystyle E_{1,2}(x_{M-1},y;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x_{M-1},y;\theta).

From now on, we will derive upper bounds for terms
\displaystyle E_{1,1}(x,x_{M-1};\theta)

and

\displaystyle E_{1,2}(x_{M-1},y;\theta)
. From
Iguchi and Yamada 2021a
, we have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|E_{1,1}(x,x_{M-1};\theta)\right|\leq C\frac{h(\Delta)}{\left((M-1)\delta_{M}\right)^{d_{R}/2}}e^{-c\frac{|x_{M-1}-x|^{2}}{(M-1)\delta_{M}}}

(H.6)

for a non-decreasing function
\displaystyle h(\cdot)
and constants
\displaystyle C,c>0
independent of
\displaystyle x,x_{M-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
.
We consider the term
\displaystyle E_{1,2}(x_{M-1},y;\theta)
. First, we note that there exists a random variable
\displaystyle\pi_{e}(t,x,B_{t};\theta)
,
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
,
\displaystyle t>0
,
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
such that for any appropriate test functions
\displaystyle f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}f(y)\bar{p}_{e,t}^{\,\mathrm{I}}(x,y;\theta)dy

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}f(y){p}_{e,t}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM}}}(x,y;\theta)\bigl\{1+\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(t,x,y;\theta)\bigr\}dy

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[f(\widetilde{X}_{e,t}^{\mathrm{EM},x})\bigl\{1+\pi_{e}(t,x,B_{t};\theta)\bigr\}\bigr],

where
\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{e,t}^{\mathrm{EM},x}
is the one-step Euler-Maruyama scheme with step-size
\displaystyle t
starting from
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
.
The stochastic weight
\displaystyle\pi_{e}(t,x,B_{t};\theta)
is interpreted as the infinite-dimensional version of the weight function

\displaystyle y\mapsto\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(t,x,y;\theta)
and is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\pi_{e}(t,x,B_{t};\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(t,x,\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},x}_{e,t};\theta)

(H.7)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\,\,=\bigg\{\,\tfrac{\sqrt{t}}{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{k_{1}}V_{k_{2}}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)V_{k_{1}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)V_{k_{2}}^{i_{3}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})}^{\widetilde{Y}_{e}}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{t}{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}\Bigl(\hat{V}_{k}V_{0}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)+\hat{V}_{0}V_{k}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\Bigr)V_{k}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}^{\widetilde{Y}_{e}}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{t}{4}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{k_{1}}V_{k_{2}}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)\hat{V}_{k_{1}}V_{k_{2}}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}^{\widetilde{Y}_{e}}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}(\xi;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{\sqrt{t^{3}}}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{R}}\hat{V}_{0}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\mathcal{H}^{\widetilde{Y}_{e}}_{(i)}(\xi;\theta)\,\bigg\}\bigg|_{\xi=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}V_{k}(x,\theta)B_{k,t}},

where we used:

\displaystyle\displaystyle m_{x,\theta,\sqrt{t},1}(\widetilde{X}_{e,t}^{\mathrm{EM},x})=\frac{\widetilde{X}_{e,t}^{\mathrm{EM},x}-x-V_{0}(x,\theta)t}{\sqrt{t}}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{R}}V_{k}(x,\theta)B_{k,t}.

We define
\displaystyle\Gamma:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle\Gamma(z)\equiv\exp\left(K(z)\right)-(1+z)
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x,y;\theta)
,
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
,
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
, is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x,y;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=p_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM}}}(x,y;\theta)\cdot\Gamma\bigl(\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\delta_{M},x,y;\theta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=p_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM}}}(x,y;\theta)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)|\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x}=y\right]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\delta_{y}(\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x})\,\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)\right],

where we have used (
H.7
) in the second line and (
E.5
) in the last line.
Using the Malliavin integration by parts formula and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle|\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x,y;\theta)|

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x}\geq y}H_{(1,\ldots,d_{R})}\bigl(\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x},\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)\bigr]\right|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x}\geq y}\bigr\|_{2}\bigl\|H_{(1,\ldots,d_{R})}\bigl(\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x},\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)\bigr\|_{2}.

From Lemma F.3 in
Iguchi and Yamada 2021a
, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x}\geq y}\bigr\|_{2}\leq h(\Delta)e^{-c\tfrac{|y-x|^{2}}{\delta_{M}}},

where
\displaystyle h(\cdot)
is a non-decreasing function and
\displaystyle c>0
is some constant. Furthermore, the upper bound (
E.1
) with Kusuoka-Stroock’s estimate (Theorem 1.20 in
Kusuoka and Stroock 1984
) yields

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl\|H_{(1,\ldots,d_{R})}\bigl(\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x},\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{(\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}\|\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)\|_{d_{R},4}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}}{(\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}(\delta_{M})^{3}

for some constants
\displaystyle C_{1}=C(\Delta),C_{2}=C_{2}(\Delta)>0
that are independent of
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}\times\Theta
under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
). In the last inequality, we used the following bound:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)\|_{d_{R},4}\leq C(\delta_{M})^{3},

(H.8)

for some constant
\displaystyle C>0
independent of
\displaystyle x
and
\displaystyle\theta
.
(
H.8
) is immediately obtained from the Taylor expansion (
G.1
) and the following estimate: Let
\displaystyle j\in\mathbb{N}
,
\displaystyle p=2k,\;k\in\mathbb{N}
. Under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
), there exists a constant
\displaystyle C>0
such that for any
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}\times\Theta
and integer
\displaystyle i\geq 7
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Bigl\|\bigl\{\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr\}^{i}\Bigr\|_{j,p}\leq C(\delta_{M})^{3}.

(H.9)

Notice that the estimate (
H.9
) is immediately obtained by recursive application of Hölder’s inequality with the following estimate. There exists constant
\displaystyle C>0
independent of
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}\times\Theta
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl\|\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr\|_{k,q}\leq C\sqrt{\delta_{M}},

for any
\displaystyle k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\displaystyle q\in(1,\infty)
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x_{M-1},y;\theta)\right|\leq C(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h(\Delta)}{(\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}e^{-c\tfrac{|y-x_{M-1}|^{2}}{\delta_{M}}},

(H.10)

for a non-decreasing function
\displaystyle h(\cdot)
and constants
\displaystyle C,c>0
independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-1},y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
.
Thus, from estimates (
H.6
), (
H.10
), we obtain the upper bound for
\displaystyle E_{1}
as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|E_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h(\Delta)}{\left((M-1)\delta_{M}\right)^{d_{R}/2}(\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}e^{-c_{1}\tfrac{|x_{M-1}-x|^{2}}{(M-1)\delta_{M}}}e^{-c_{2}\tfrac{|y-x_{M-1}|^{2}}{\delta_{M}}}dx_{M-1}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h(\Delta)}{\Delta^{d_{R}/2}}e^{-c_{3}\tfrac{|y-x|^{2}}{\Delta}},

where we have made use of the following result. There exist constants
\displaystyle C,c>0
such that for any constants
\displaystyle c_{1},c_{2}>0
and
\displaystyle 0<r<s
,
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{{d_{R}}/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}e^{-c_{1}\frac{|z-x|^{2}}{r}}e^{-c_{2}\frac{|y-z|^{2}}{s-r}}dz\leq C\left(\frac{s-r}{s}\right)^{d_{R}/2}e^{-c\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{s}}.

(H.11)

The proof is now complete.

H.1.2
Upper Bound (
H.5
)

Term
\displaystyle E_{2}
is written as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle E_{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{M-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}F_{1,k}(x,x_{M-k-1};\theta)F_{2,k}(x_{M-k-1},y;\theta)dx_{M-k-1},

where

\displaystyle\displaystyle F_{1,k}(x,x_{M-k-1};\theta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}\times(M-k-1)}}\prod_{i=1}^{M-k-1}\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{I}}_{e,\delta_{M}}(x_{i-1},x_{i};\theta)dx_{1}\cdots dx_{M-k-2};

\displaystyle\displaystyle F_{2,k}(x_{M-k-1},y;\theta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}\mathscr{R}_{\delta_{M}}(x_{M-k-1},x_{M-k};\theta)\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k},y;\theta)dx_{M-k},

for
\displaystyle 1\leq j\leq M-1
, where we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k},y;\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}\times(k-1)}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II}}_{e,\delta_{M}}(x_{M-k+(i-1)},x_{M-k+i};\theta)dx_{M-k+1}\cdots dx_{M-1},

(H.12)

under the notation
\displaystyle x_{M}=y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
. From
Iguchi and Yamada 2021a
, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|F_{1,k}(x,x_{M-k-1};\theta)\right|\leq C\frac{h(\Delta)}{((M-k-1)\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}e^{-c\tfrac{|x_{M-k-1}-x|^{2}}{(M-k-1)\delta_{M}}},

for some non-decreasing function
\displaystyle h(\cdot)
and constants
\displaystyle C,c>0
independent of
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}\times\Theta
under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
).
To obtain an upper bound for
\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k-1},y;\theta)
, we will express it as a generalised expectation. First, from the Markov property of Euler-Maruyama scheme and (
H.7
) we have for a measurable function
\displaystyle f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}f(y)\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II}}_{e,\delta_{M}}(x,\xi;\theta)\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II}}_{e,\delta_{M}}(\xi,y;\theta)d\xi dy

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[f(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},\xi}_{e,\delta_{M}})\,e^{K\bigl(\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\delta_{M},\xi,\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},\xi}_{e,\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)}\bigr]\,\bar{p}^{\,\mathrm{II}}_{e,\delta_{M}}(x,\xi;\theta)\,d\xi

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[f(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},\xi}_{e,\delta_{M}})\,e^{K\bigl(\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\delta_{M},\xi,\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},\xi}_{e,\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)}\bigr]|_{\xi=\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},x}_{e,\delta_{M}}}\,\times e^{K\bigl(\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\delta_{M},x,\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},x}_{e,\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\bigl[f(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x}_{e,2\delta_{M}})\,e^{K\bigl(\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},x}_{e,\delta_{M}},\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x}_{e,2\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)}|\mathcal{F}_{\delta_{M}}\bigr]\,\times e^{K\bigl(\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\delta_{M},x,\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},x}_{e,\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[f(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x}_{e,2\delta_{M}})\,e^{K\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},x}_{e,\delta_{M}},B_{\delta_{M}}^{[2]};\theta)\bigr)}e^{K\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},x,B_{\delta_{M}}^{[1]};\theta)\bigr)}\Bigr],

(H.13)

where
\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{e,k\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x}
,
\displaystyle 0\leq k\leq M
,
is the Markov chain of Euler-Maruyama scheme at time
\displaystyle k\delta_{M}
, starting from point
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
with time step of length
\displaystyle\delta_{M}
, and
\displaystyle B^{[l]}_{\delta_{M}}:=B_{l\delta_{M}}-B_{(l-1)\delta_{M}}
,
\displaystyle 1\leq l\leq M
.
Since it follows from (
E.1
) and (
H.7
) that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{p}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\,\mathrm{II}}(x_{M-1},y;\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\delta_{y}(\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x_{M-1}})\,e^{K\bigl(\Psi_{e}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\delta_{M},x_{M-1},\widetilde{X}_{e,\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},x_{M-1}};\theta)\bigr)}\Bigr],

(H.14)

(
H.14
) and recursive application of (
H.1.2
) yield:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k},y;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigl[\delta_{y}(\widetilde{X}_{e,k\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}})\,\prod_{l=1}^{k}e^{K\bigl(\pi_{e}\bigl(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(l-1)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B^{[M-k+l]}_{\delta_{M}};\theta\bigr)\bigr)}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\delta_{y}(\widetilde{X}_{e,k\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}})\,G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\right].

Use of the Malliavin integration by parts formula and Hölder’s inequality gives:

\displaystyle\displaystyle|\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k},y;\theta)|

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}}_{e,k\delta_{M}}\geq y}H_{(1,\ldots,d_{R})}\bigl(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}}_{e,k\delta_{M}},G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k};\theta}\bigr)]\Bigr|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}}_{e,k\delta_{M}}\geq y}\bigr\|_{2}\left\|H_{(1,\ldots,d_{R})}\bigl(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}}_{e,k\delta_{M}},G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k};\theta}\bigr)\right\|_{2}.

By Lemma F.1 in
Iguchi and Yamada 2021a
again, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}}_{e,k\delta_{M}}\geq y}\bigr\|_{2}\leq h(\Delta)e^{-c\frac{y-x_{M-k}}{k\delta_{M}}},

for a non-decreasing function
\displaystyle h(\cdot)
and a constant
\displaystyle c>0
that are independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-k},y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
). The upper bound (
E.1
) with Kusuoka-Stroock’s estimate (Theorem 1.20 in
Kusuoka and Stroock 1984
) yields that there exist constants
\displaystyle C_{1},C_{2},c>0
independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-k},y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\|H_{(1,\ldots,d_{R})}\bigl(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}}_{e,k\delta_{M}},G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\bigr)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{C_{1}}{\bigl(k\delta_{M}\bigr)^{d_{R}/2}}\left\|G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\right\|_{d_{R},4}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{C_{2}}{\bigl(k\delta_{M}\bigr)^{d_{R}/2}},

where on the last inequality we have made use of the estimate derived as follows. Let
\displaystyle j,k\in\mathbb{N}
. Under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
), there exists a constant
\displaystyle C=C(\Delta)>0
independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-k}
and
\displaystyle\theta
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\|_{j,2k}\leq C.

(H.15)

We will give the proof of (
H.15
) in the end of this subsection. Thus, the upper bound of
\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k},y;\theta)
is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle|\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k},y;\theta)|\leq C\frac{h(\Delta)}{(k\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}e^{-c\tfrac{|y-x_{M-k}|^{2}}{k\delta_{M}}},

for a non-decreasing constant
\displaystyle h(\cdot)
and some constants
\displaystyle C,c>0
independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-k},y
and
\displaystyle\theta
. Combining the estimate of
\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{2,k}(x_{M-k},y;\theta)
with that of (
H.10
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\displaystyle|F_{2,k}(x_{M-k-1},y;\theta)|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h(\Delta)}{(\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}(k\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}e^{-c_{1}\tfrac{|x_{M-k}-x_{M-k-1}|^{2}}{\delta_{M}}}e^{-c_{2}\tfrac{|y-x_{M-k}|^{2}}{k\delta_{M}}}dx_{M-k}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h(\Delta)}{((k+1)\delta_{M})^{d_{R}/2}}e^{-c_{3}\tfrac{|y-x_{M-k-1}|^{2}}{(k+1)\delta_{M}}},

(H.16)

for some positive constants
\displaystyle C_{1},C_{2},c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}
independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-k-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
, where we used estimate (
H.11
) on the last inequality. Finally, thanks to (
H.11
) and the upper bounds (
H.12
), (
H.1.2
), we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle|E_{2}|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{M-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}}|F_{1,k}(x_{M-k-1};\theta)||F_{2,k}(x_{M-k-1};\theta)|dx_{M-k-1}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C(M-1)(\delta_{M})^{3}\frac{h(\Delta)}{\Delta^{d_{R}/2}}e^{-c\tfrac{|y-x|^{2}}{\Delta}},

for a non-decreasing function
\displaystyle h(\Delta)
and some constants
\displaystyle C,c>0
independent of
\displaystyle x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
. The proof is now complete.

(
Proof of
(
H.15
)).
Recall that
\displaystyle\Gamma(z):=e^{K(z)}-(1+z),\,z\in\mathbb{R}
, with Taylor expansion (
G.1
). We have

\displaystyle\displaystyle G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}=\prod_{l=1}^{k}e^{K\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(l-1)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B^{[M-k+l]}_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\prod_{l=1}^{k}\Bigl\{1+\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(l-1)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B^{[M-k+l]}_{\delta_{M}};\theta)+\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(l-1)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B^{[M-k+l]}_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\bigr)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\equiv\prod_{l=1}^{k}g^{l}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}.

Similarly, we define

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\prod_{l=1}^{k}\Bigl\{1+\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(l-1)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B^{[M-k+l]}_{\delta_{M}};\theta)\Bigr\}\equiv\prod_{l=1}^{k}\widetilde{g}^{l}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}.

Note that for any
\displaystyle j,e\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|{G}^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\|_{j,2e}\leq\|\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\|_{j,2e}+\|G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}-\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\|_{j,2e}.

(H.17)

Due to Lemma 2 in
Iguchi and Yamada 2022
, the first term of the right-hand side of (
H.17
)
is bounded by some positive constant
\displaystyle C=C(\Delta)
independent of
\displaystyle(x_{M-k},\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{R}}\times\Theta
under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
).
We consider the second term of the right hand side of (
H.17
). Since it holds

\displaystyle\displaystyle G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}-\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{k}\biggl\{\prod_{l=1}^{k-m}g^{l}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\biggr\}\,\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(k-m)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B_{\delta_{M}}^{[M-m+1]};\theta)\bigr)\,\biggl\{\prod_{l=1}^{m-1}\widetilde{g}^{k-l+1}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\biggr\},

we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|G^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}-\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\|_{j,2e}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{k}\left\|\prod_{l=1}^{k-m}g^{l}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\right\|_{j,p}\bigl\|\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(k-m)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B_{\delta_{M}}^{[M-m+1]};\theta)\bigr)\bigr\|_{j,q}\left\|\prod_{l=1}^{m-1}\widetilde{g}^{k-l+1}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\right\|_{j,r},

for some positive even integers
\displaystyle p,q,r
, where we used Hölder’s inequality.
Under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
), Lemma 2 in
Iguchi and Yamada 2022
and the uniformly boundedness property of the function
\displaystyle z\mapsto\exp(K(z))
yield

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{l=1}^{k-m}g^{l}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\right\|_{j,p}\leq C_{1},\quad\left\|\prod_{l=1}^{m-1}\widetilde{g}^{k-l+1}_{\delta_{M},x_{M-k},\theta}\right\|_{j,r}\leq C_{2},\ \ 1\leq m\leq k,

for some constants
\displaystyle C_{1},C_{2}>0
independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-k}
and
\displaystyle\theta
.
Furthermore, applying a similar discussion as the one for obtaining the bound (
H.8
), we have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Bigl\|\Gamma\bigl(\pi_{e}(\delta_{M},\widetilde{X}_{e,(k-m)\delta_{M}}^{\mathrm{EM},(M),x_{M-k}},B_{\delta_{M}}^{[M-m+1]};\theta)\bigr)\Bigr\|_{j,q}\leq C(\delta_{M})^{3},

for some constant
\displaystyle C>0
independent of
\displaystyle x_{M-k}
and
\displaystyle\theta
under conditions (
H1
)–(
H2
).
The proof is now complete.

Appendix I
Additional Simulation Study Under High Frequency Observations Regime

We consider the stochastic FitzHugh-Nagumo model used to describe the dynamics of action potential generation within an neuronal axon. The model is specified as a
\displaystyle 2
-dimensional hypo-elliptic SDE driven by a scalar Brownian motion (
\displaystyle d=2
,
\displaystyle d_{S}=d_{R}=1
):

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{aligned} dX_{t}&=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}dX_{R,t}\\[2.84544pt]
dX_{S,t}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma X_{S,t}-X_{R,t}+\alpha\\[2.84544pt]
\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\bigl(X_{S,t}-(X_{S,t})^{3}-X_{R,t}-s\bigr)\end{array}\right]dt+\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\sigma\\[2.84544pt]
0\end{array}\right]dB_{t},\\
\end{aligned}

(I.1)

where
\displaystyle\gamma,\alpha,\varepsilon,s,\sigma
are the parameters. Typically,
\displaystyle s
is fixed in experiments, thus we will estimate parameter
\displaystyle\theta=(\gamma,\alpha,\varepsilon,\sigma)
under the complete observations regime by making use of new contrast estimator and compare it against the local Gaussian contrast estimator proposed by
Gloter and Yoshida 2020
. The
\displaystyle\Phi_{2}
term in the new contrast (
4.2
) for the model (
I.1
) is given as: for
\displaystyle\Delta>0,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2},x=[x_{R},x_{S}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)=-\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\times\mathcal{H}_{(1,1)}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)+\Bigl\{\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{2\varepsilon}-\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{6\varepsilon^{2}}\bigl(1-3(x_{S})^{2}\bigr)\Bigr\}\times\mathcal{H}_{(1,2)}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\Bigl\{-\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{8\varepsilon^{2}}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{8\varepsilon^{3}}\bigl(1-3(x_{S})^{2}\bigr)\Bigr\}\times\mathcal{H}_{(2,2)}(\Delta,x,y;\theta),

with the Hermite polynomials
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{(i_{1},i_{2})}(\Delta,x,y;\theta)\,1\leq i_{1},i_{2}\leq 2
defined in (
3.20
) in the main text.
In our experiment, we fix
\displaystyle s=0.01
and set the true parameter to

\theta^{\dagger}=(\gamma^{\dagger},\alpha^{\dagger},\varepsilon^{\dagger},\sigma)=(1.50,0.30,0.10,0.60).

We generate ‘correct’ synthetic datasets
\displaystyle Y_{\mathrm{FN}}
over the time period
\displaystyle[0,100]
under
\displaystyle\theta^{\dagger}
by using the local Gaussian scheme (
2.6
) given in the main text with very small discretisation step
\displaystyle 10^{-4}
. We will consider the following three scenarios for the number of datasets
\displaystyle n
and the observation time step
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}
, by subsampling from the synthetic datasets
\displaystyle Y_{\mathrm{FN}}
:

FN-1.

\displaystyle(n,\Delta_{n})=(5000,0.02)
with the time interval of observations
\displaystyle T\equiv n\Delta_{n}=100
.

FN-2.

\displaystyle(n,\Delta_{n})=(10000,0.01)
with
\displaystyle T=100
.

FN-3.

\displaystyle(n,\Delta_{n})=(20000,0.005)
with
\displaystyle T=100
.

We use the adaptive moments (Adam) optimiser to minimise the contrast estimators with the following algorithmic specifications:
(step-size) =
\displaystyle 0.01
, (exponential decay rate for the first moment estimates) =
\displaystyle 0.9
, (exponential decay rate for the second moment estimates) =
\displaystyle 0.999
, (additive term for numerical stability) =
\displaystyle 1\times 10^{-8}
and (number of iterations) =
\displaystyle 20,000
.
In Table
4
, we summarise the mean and standard deviation of the two contrast estimators as computed by
\displaystyle 20
trajectories under experimental design
FN-3
.
For the drift parameters, the local Gaussian and the new contrast estimators provide similar accuracy, and give a very accurate estimation for
\displaystyle\varepsilon
, i.e. the parameter at the drift of the smooth component, as also anticipated by the rapid convergence rate
\displaystyle\textstyle{\Delta_{n}^{1/2}{n}^{-1/2}}
towards normality in Theorem
4.2
.
Estimation of the diffusion parameter
\displaystyle\sigma
is improved via use of the new contrast. We focus on the estimation of
\displaystyle\sigma
. In Figure
3
, we plot the root mean squared errors (RMSEs) for the two contrast estimators for
\displaystyle\sigma
computed by
\displaystyle 20
replicates under the settings
FN-1,2,3
. The figure illustrates that the new estimator gives faster convergence to the true parameter than the local Gaussian contrast, which (arguably) relates to the weaker requirement of
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/3})
versus
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
to obtain asymptotic normality for the estimators provided by the analytical results in this work – see Theorem
4.2
and Remark
4.2
in the main text.

:
Mean and standard deviation (in bracket) from
\displaystyle 20
parameter estimator replicates in the stochastic FitzHugh-Nagumo model (
I.1
) under scenario
FN-3
.

\displaystyle\alpha

:
Root mean squared errors (RMSEs) obtained from
\displaystyle 20
replications of estimates for
\displaystyle\sigma
in the stochastic FitzHugh-Nagumo model (
I.1
), under scenarios
FN-1,2,3
.
